(d) *Payment reversal:* `reverse_payment` (`payroll.reverse_payment`, P; approver, never the recorder or approver) reverses an approved payment through `ledger.reverse_entry`; a paid run or settlement goes back to `approved`; a reversed remuneration payment is a negative WHT row in the reversal month. (e) *Leave accrual:* not built (hr OQ-6 undecided; 2350 / 5130 approved only "if leave is accrued monthly"); `leave_liability_report` gives the liability (balance × actual daily wage), nothing posted. (f) *Hourly GOSI (Regs Art. 18(3)):* hr contracts state `pay_rate_basis`; a daily-rated contract's GOSI wage is the monthly equivalent (daily rate × 30, Art. 18(1)); an hourly-rated one is refused while `gosi.variable_wage_basis` `hourly` is `not_configured` (unverified) **[CC-verify: Governor's hourly rules not supplied]**; the only configurable method is `monthly_equivalent`.
5. **WHT on remuneration in the WHT return (R43, OQ-30).** `invoicing.services.register_wht_return_source` (a small hook in invoicing, tested there) lets payroll add its rows to `invoicing.wht_return_data`: one row per WHT category, payee (employee code only) and rate, the payment's SAR base (`PayrollPayment.wht_base_sar`, at the payment-date rate) and WHT split over the run's remuneration payslips by net pay (parts add up exactly); `source` `payroll`, `payee_kind` `employee`. The 2220 tie-out now counts payroll payment entries. OQ-29 (category and rate) stays open: a category is still required on every remuneration payment.
6. **hr R68 and R69:** see hr.md "As built: payroll slice 2 additions".
7. **Egypt payroll (R4, R41; OQ-28; `docs/compliance/egypt-payroll-2026-10-18.md`).** Regular in-country runs of an EG entity pay its `local_entity_employee` staff in EGP from EG rows only: prorated contract earnings, unpaid days, approved inputs (one-off earnings, bonus, one-off deductions with basis written consent, court order, maintenance (`nafaqa`) order or other statutory; overtime refused until its base is confirmed); social insurance 11% / 18.75% on the insurable wage (components in `si.insurable_components`) clamped to the year's `si.wage_limits` (a year without its own row is refused: NOSI publishes them each November), prorated in joining and leaving months (`si.partial_month`); Universal Health Insurance (employee 1%, employer 4% with EGP 50 minimum) when `PayrollSettings.work_governorate` is in `uhi.governorates_live` (family contributions not computed: warning); Martyrs' Fund 0.05% of the gross (employee-borne default, research C8); salary tax by cumulative annualised withholding (`tax.salary.method`) on the month's pay less the employee's social insurance, the personal exemption, the brackets with the high-income exclusion table (`tax.salary.bracket_exclusion`, column chosen by the whole annual income) and the residents-only zero band; no EOSB accrual (`eosb.monthly_accrual` false); Egypt caps on the wage after tax and insurance (nafaqa ≤ 50% and first, other debts ≤ 25% and ≤ 50% with the nafaqa, loans ≤ 10%; a fixed deduction above refuses the payslip); no WPS, Mudad or GOSI alerts. Accounts (defaults on the group chart, **CFO to approve dedicated Egypt accounts**): social insurance 2340 / 5110, salary tax 2220, Martyrs' Fund and UHI payable 2300, UHI employer 5110. **Activation:** a run is calculated (with a warning counting the unverified rows) but refused at submission and approval while any EG row it read is not verified, whatever `HR_REQUIRE_VERIFIED_PARAMETERS` says. Off-cycle Egypt runs and Egypt terminations are not built; payments to NOSI and ETA have no payment kind yet (open item).
8. **Bai' ajel through invoicing (R47 to R49, E12, E13; invoicing R112).** `PAYROLL_INVOICING_PORT` is `invoicing.payroll_port.PayrollInvoicingPort`. Sale approval sets the sale date before calling the port; the contract is `sale_invoiced` until invoicing reports the invoice posted (ZATCA reporting), then `sold` (`_refresh_deferred_sales`, run before every calculation and by the daily alerts job); only then are installments deducted. A rejected invoice is approved again (a new invoice, same price and schedule); never while the invoice is live. Each installment a run approval collects calls `recognise_bai_ajel_profit` (Dr 1241 / Cr 4610 by collected share, never by time, posted by the bai' ajel system user after the person's approval; dated the run's entry date); a run reversal calls `reverse_bai_ajel_profit`; a final-settlement recovery recognises its share (the collection completing the receivable releases the rest, E13). Payroll's own periodic `DeferredProfitRecognition` (E12 straight-line) is therefore not built: recognition follows collections (invoicing's design, owner decision A6; which governs, on-collection or E12's periodic straight line and E13's "continues per schedule" after an unrecovered exit, is invoicing **OQ-75**, default on collection). Payroll never posts Dr 1241 / Cr 4610 itself: only `recognise_bai_ajel_profit` does, so nothing is booked twice; `payroll.prepare_profit_recognition` / `approve_profit_recognition` are seeded for invoicing's `COLLECTING_APPROVALS` and a later periodic catch-up.

**Defaults chosen in slice 2 (team recommendations):** correction entries dated the approval day; the write-off account 5100 until the CFO names one; cost items capped at the source's cost net of input VAT; Egypt accounts on the group chart; Egypt salary tax without the unconfirmed ETA rounding rule; Martyrs' Fund employee-borne; profit recognised on collection.

### 10.9 Code-review and compliance fixes of slice 2 (2026-10-20, as built)
Built on the owner's instruction "proceed on the recommendations, no questions". Regression tests: `payroll/test_review_fixes_2026_10_20.py` (fake invoicing port) and `invoicing/test_deferred_sale_review.py` (the real port); the profit tests of `invoicing/test_deferred_sale.py` now collect through payroll's marker. Migrations `payroll.0017` (asset type, rebate, correction due date, one open correction, WHT parts; PostgreSQL guards), `invoicing.0031` (sale deactivation only on rejection; bill guard), `invoicing.0032` (adjustment model and guard).

Code review:
1. **The asset's purchase bill stays bought (invoicing R124).** `invoicing.reverse_document` refuses (`bai_ajel_asset_bill`, en/ar) a bill backing an active `DeferredSale` (issued, rejected-and-not-yet-replaced or posted) or recorded as a contract's acquisition (`purchase_bill`, any status but a future `discarded`); a vendor credit note with a line on 1250 against such a bill is refused at submission and approval. Bills are reversed only through `reverse_document` (registered `reverse_guard`). `record_asset_acquisition` locks the bill row. PostgreSQL: `invoicing_bai_ajel_bill_lock` (sale) and `invoicing_bai_ajel_contract_lock` (contract, from `payroll.0017`; named to fire before invoicing's document lock). A contract whose acquisition must be undone has no withdrawal path yet (open item).
2. **Final settlement and a sale awaiting reporting.** `prepare_final_settlement` and `approve_final_settlement` call `_refresh_deferred_sales` first (a sale invoice posted since becomes `sold`, so its `deferred_sale_recovery` line is computed) and refuse (`sale_not_posted`, en/ar, naming the contract) while any contract of the leaver is `sale_invoiced`.
3. **1240, 1241 and 4610 are restricted accounts** (`ledger.restricted`, registered by invoicing R127): 1240 only by the bai' ajel sale invoice, its payroll-approved adjustment credit note (A8) and payroll's `PayrollRun` and `FinalSettlement` entries (registered in `payroll/apps.py`); 1241 by the sale invoice, the adjustment and `DeferredProfitRelease`; 4610 by `DeferredProfitRelease` only; reversals follow their original. Manual journals are refused (drafts too). The migration opening-entry allowance (`migration`, every restricted account) still applies: opening balances of existing bai' ajel receivables load through the opening entry (open item for the CFO).
4. **Profit is recognised only for a collection payroll approved.** `approve_run` and `approve_final_settlement` apply their effects inside `payroll.invoicing_port.collecting(document, user)`; `reverse_run` gives profit back inside the same marker in `reverse` mode. `invoicing.recognise_deferred_profit` / `reverse_deferred_profit` call `payroll.invoicing_port.collection_problems`: refused (`collection_not_from_payroll`, en/ar, logged denial) unless the marker is set for that user, `source_ref` is `payroll.deferredsaleinstallment:<pk>` of that very contract, the installment is `deducted` by that run (or `settled_in_final` by a settlement of that employee) and the amount is its `deducted_amount`; the user must then hold `payroll.approve_run` (run), `payroll.approve_final_settlement` (settlement) or `payroll.reverse_run` (reversal). A CFO (or anyone) calling invoicing directly is refused. `payroll.approve_profit_recognition` no longer opens recognition (a later periodic catch-up would need its own marker, OQ-75).
5. **Cost item sources (R63).** A source bill is posted with no `reversal_entry`; a source journal entry is posted, not a reversal and not reversed. The cost it booked is its debits on **expense accounts and prepayments (1320)** only (balance-sheet debits such as vehicles, receivables or bank never count). Checked at creation and again at approval, each time under a row lock of the source (bill or entry) so two items never both fit; an approved item whose source was reversed later is left out of the secondment cost report with an en/ar warning naming it.
6. **One open correction per settlement:** the settlement row is locked before the check, and the database constraint `payroll_one_open_correction` (partial unique on `settlement` for `draft`, `submitted`, `returned`) refuses a second one under concurrency (`correction_open`).
7. **WHT on final-settlement payments (R43).** A payment of a final settlement of a `remuneration` person needs `wht_category` (`wht_category_required`; today H17 keeps them out of final settlements, so the rule guards a future basis); settlement payments with WHT go into the WHT return rows (`source` `payroll`) and the 2220 tie-out matches.
8. **Remuneration retro across a currency change:** an earlier month whose contract currency (as approved now) or paid currency differs from the payslip's is never compared across currencies: unchanged in its own currency it needs nothing; any difference, or a back-dated currency change, refuses the payslip (en/ar, naming the month and currencies; correct by an approved input in the new currency).
9. See invoicing R123 as amended (deactivation only on rejection).
10. **PDPL (invoicing OQ-74, DPO):** the employee's name is no longer copied into the invoicing customer record (name = `EMP-<code>`); `invoicing.view_customer` shows the hr name unmasked only to people holding `invoicing.view_personal_data` or `payroll.view_payslip` in the entity, each view logged (`personal_data_viewed`); everyone else sees the mask.

Compliance (recommended defaults):
- **A1:** the simplified sale invoice is given to the employee after ZATCA reported it (contract `sold`), printed or electronic; `record_sale_invoice_delivery(contract, *, user, channel, reference)` (`payroll.create_deferred_sale`) logs the delivery (AccessLog `bai_ajel_invoice_delivered` with the invoice number and channel). Nothing is sent externally.
- **A2:** VAT stays on the whole sale price including the profit (D33(i)); the adviser question is invoicing OQ-78.
- **A3:** `record_asset_acquisition(..., asset_type=...)` requires `AssetType` (vehicle, equipment, electronics, furniture, other movable goods); `real_estate` is refused (en/ar): a real-estate sale is VAT-exempt and subject to RETT (5%), not the 15% sale invoice (invoicing R125, OQ-77). Invoicing refuses it again.
- **A6:** a `sale_date` after today (Riyadh) is refused in payroll and in invoicing (`sale_date_future`).
- **A8 (minimal compliant path chosen: the ibra' rebate; returns are not built):** `prepare_deferred_sale_rebate(contract, *, user, amount, reason_ar, request_ref)` (payroll officer; the employee's written request; contract `sold`; one open rebate) → `approve_deferred_sale_rebate` (`payroll.approve_deferred_sale`, A, P, matrix row `payroll.deferred_sale`; never the preparer or the employee): invoicing issues a simplified **credit note** on the sale invoice through the port (one 1241 line at the rebate, VAT 15%; posts on reporting Dr 1241 / Dr 2200 / Cr 1240), never above the profit not yet recognised; payroll reduces the planned instalments by its gross from the last one back (a rebate above what is still to be deducted is refused: an ibra' never pays money out). Profit recognition then uses the profit and receivable less the active adjustments, so the total recognised is exactly the fixed profit less the rebate. A rejected credit note is replaced by approving the rebate again. A return of the asset is not built (refused: no credit note path exists for it). Whether a rebate may be granted at all stays **[Needs expert sign-off: Sharia]** (OQ-33).
- **B2:** the WHT base is the gross remuneration of the payslips (the settlement's gross for a settlement payment) in the share of the open net the payment pays, not the net paid; R43 below reads accordingly.
- **B3:** WHT only for non-residents: a payee with `country_of_residence` SA bears no WHT; the payment carries an en/ar warning (hr R52 already refuses a KSA residence for remuneration staff, so this is a second check). Per-payee parts are stored on the payment (`wht_parts`) and are the WHT return rows; the payslip shows WHT on the gross (0 for a resident).
- **B5:** a reversed payment keeps its negative row in the reversal month, flagged `reversal`, `amends_return_month` (the original payment month) and an en/ar note "amend the WHT return of <month>"; invoicing's vendor WHT reversal rows carry the same flag.
- **C1:** an approved correction that increases what is due gets `due_date` = the approval day and an immediate alert to the payroll officers and approvers (`fsc:<pk>:due`); the daily alerts job sends a late alert the day after while the settlement's due amount is unpaid.
- **C2:** the GOSI report lists correction lines under `corrections` with their origin month (the settlement's leaving month) and an en/ar note "correction of <month>, counted in <month>"; they still count in the month of their entry.
- **D1:** the fine estimate is 2% a month (or part) of the contributions **paid late** (booked for the period less GOSI payments dated on or before the due day), never more than 100% of them; the stored note and the GOSI report state the 100% cap and the full waiver for a delay of up to 10 days (Regs Art. 25). GOSI's invoice still governs.
- **E5:** Egypt's 25% cap for other debts is computed on the wage after tax, insurance and the employer's loan instalments (the nafaqa 50% cap and the 10% loan cap unchanged).
- **E6:** see hr.md (Egypt minimum wage warning).

Second code review of the ibra' path and WHT (2026-10-20, as built; tests `payroll/test_review_fixes_2026_10_20b.py` (fake port) and `invoicing/test_deferred_sale_review2.py` (real port); migration `payroll.0018`):
11. **The rebate credit note comes only from the rebate approval (finding 1; invoicing R128(a)).** `approve_deferred_sale_rebate` calls the port inside `invoicing_port.rebating(rebate, user)`; invoicing refuses `create_deferred_sale_adjustment` otherwise, or for another rebate, contract, amount or person, or for the preparer or the employee (`rebate_not_from_payroll`, logged). The fake test port checks the same marker.
12. **A rejected credit note goes back to payroll (finding 2; invoicing R128(b), (c)).** Only a final credit note changes invoicing's figures. `DeferredSaleRebate` adds status `rejected`, `credit_note_status` and `installment_cuts` (the planned instalments the approval reduced, by period). `_refresh_rebates` (with `_refresh_deferred_sales`: run calculation, final settlement, the daily alerts job; and the approval itself) reads each approved rebate's note through `rebate_state`: `final` is recorded (never read again); `rejected` restores the cuts (into the planned instalment of the period, a recreated one, or the period after the last instalment when that period was already collected), sets `rejected` and alerts (`PayrollAlert` kind `bai_ajel_rebate`, en/ar, the note and contract numbers) the payroll officers and the rebate's approver. A `rejected` rebate is open (one open rebate per contract: `draft` or `rejected`, constraint and service); it is approved again with four eyes (new credit note, instalments reduced again) or discarded. PostgreSQL `payroll_rebate_lock` (0018): approved → final or → rejected (only from a non-final note), rejected → approved or discarded; nothing else.
13. **WHT base per payee (finding 3; R43 note).** `record_payment(..., payslips=[...])` names the payslips a salary payment pays (`payslip_ids`; `payslips_invalid` for another run, currency or a refused slip; `overpayment` above their open net); a part payment of a run with remuneration payslips must name them (`payees_required`, en/ar). At approval `payslip_parts` stores what it paid on each (pro rata to what is open on each covered payslip, rounded to the currency's decimals, largest remainder, adding up to the payment); the WHT base of a remuneration payee is gross × paid on their payslip ÷ their net. A payment approved before `0018` (no parts) counts pro rata to the nets when later payments read what is open.
14. **WHT parts backfill (finding 4).** `payroll.0018` rebuilds `wht_parts` of WHT payments approved (or reversed) before `0017` from what was booked (the SAR base and the WHT on 2220 unchanged): a settlement payment's employee; a run payment's remuneration payslips in its currency split by net (the old base was the net paid), parts marked `backfilled`. The approved-payment lock is disabled for that one update inside the migration's transaction. The WHT return source refuses (`wht_parts_missing`, en/ar, naming the payments) while any WHT payment in the month has no parts.
15. **VAT rate of the rebate note (finding 5; invoicing R128(d)):** the original sale invoice line's rate.
16. **Tests (item 6):** a rebate approved after a run (or a final settlement) was calculated is refused at approval by the fingerprint (`recalculate`), then recalculated with the reduced instalment; a real April run after a reported rebate credits 1240 with the instalment and recognises profit at the reduced figures.

17. **Rejection read after the final settlement (third review 2026-10-20, finding 1; migration `payroll.0019`).** Once the employee's final settlement is approved (or paid) no run deducts an instalment again, so `_restore_rebate_cuts` brings each cut back as an `outstanding_after_exit` instalment (period of the cut, `carried_from` the instalment of that period, or the contract's last one), on 1240 like any balance the final dues did not cover (R49, AT-32); the `bai_ajel_rebate` alert says so (en/ar: outstanding after exit on 1240, recover it from the former employee, discard the rebate). Such a rebate is not approved again (`rebate_after_exit`, en/ar, refused before invoicing is asked: no instalment is left to reduce; the after-exit instalments are immutable, PostgreSQL `payroll_installment_lock`); it is discarded. Before the settlement is approved a rejection restores planned instalments as before (the settlement's approval fingerprint then asks for a recalculation, item 16).
18. **Transactions (finding 2).** `send_alerts` (the `payroll_alerts` command) runs each entity in `transaction.atomic()` and `_refresh_deferred_sales` / `_refresh_rebates` are atomic themselves (the bai' ajel refresh takes row locks, which PostgreSQL refuses in autocommit), and each rejection's restore, status and alerts commit together (a failing alert leaves the rebate `approved` with its cuts, read again next time).
19. **`payroll_rebate_lock` tightened (finding 3; `payroll.0019`, `0018` unchanged).** Never deleted; a draft changes freely; approved: the note becomes `final` once (from a note not final; nothing else changes), or the rebate becomes `rejected` (only from a note not final; status and note state only); a final note's rebate never changes; rejected: only the one-time token changes while an approval is in progress, approved again only with a new credit note (`credit_note_ref` changed and not empty, `credit_note_status` `issued` or `final`, an approver, the token cleared; amount and reason locked), or discarded (status only); a discarded rebate never changes. Raw-SQL tests for every transition on PostgreSQL.
20. **The rebating marker is bound to the approval (finding 4; invoicing R128(a)).** `approve_deferred_sale_rebate` writes a one-time random token (`approval_token`) on the locked rebate row in its transaction, calls the port inside `rebating(rebate, user, token=..., grant=...)` and clears the token when done. `rebate_problems` reads the rebate row again (the approval holds its lock) and requires the token to match, the rebate `draft` or `rejected` at that amount, four eyes as the approval (never the preparer, the last modifier or the employee), the approver's own grant for that contract (the logged grant, valid and unused, `verify_grant`) and the authority matrix (`approvals.check`, row `payroll.deferred_sale`). An object with the rebate's pk alone issues nothing.
21. **Backfilled WHT rows flagged (finding 6).** In the WHT return the rows from parts `payroll.0018` rebuilt are kept apart from live rows and flagged `review_required` with `review_en` / `review_ar` (rebuilt from the WHT booked on the named payments, split by net pay: check before filing), naming the reason when the payee is recorded as resident in KSA (`review_reasons` `resident`, B3) or is not paid as remuneration staff (`not_remuneration`, B2). They are not dropped: the return still ties to 2220 as booked; the correction (amendment or refund) is a human decision.
22. **WHT tests and a zero-net payslip (item 7).** Tests: a payment approved before `0018` (no parts) counts pro rata in `_payslip_paid`; a KSA resident named by a payment bears no WHT (warning) and does not appear in the return. A payslip with no net pay (all deducted) is covered at 0 by a payment and no WHT is withheld for it from that payment (before: its whole gross was in the base, withheld from the other payees' money); the payment carries an en/ar warning naming the payee and the gross to record with the tax adviser.

Fourth review of the ibra' path and payroll WHT (2026-10-21, as built; tests `payroll/test_review_fixes_2026_10_21.py` (fake port, ported from the reviewer's rv4 probes), `payroll/test_pg_review_2026_10_21.py` (PostgreSQL) and `invoicing/test_deferred_sale_review4.py` (real port); migration `payroll.0020`):
23. **WHT capped at the payee's own part of the payment (item 1; R43, OQ-29).** The zero-net fix of item 22 was a threshold: a payee with a net of 0.01 still had WHT on the whole gross withheld, funded by the other payees. Now, for every payee (run or settlement payment, any net), the WHT withheld is at most their own part of the payment (`payslip_parts`; the payment amount for a settlement). Where the WHT due on their base is more, the withheld part keeps the matching share of the base in `wht_parts` (base × withheld ÷ due) and the rest (base and WHT not withheld, in the payment currency and SAR) goes to `PayrollPayment.wht_uncovered`, with an en/ar warning naming the payee, the WHT due, what was withheld and what was not ("no net pay" when nothing was). Who bears the WHT not withheld is for the tax adviser (OQ-29); nothing of it is booked on 2220.
24. **Review row in the WHT return (item 6).** `_wht_return_source` adds, per category, payee and rate, a row for `wht_uncovered` with `wht_sar` 0 (so the return still ties to 2220), `wht_not_withheld_sar`, `not_withheld`, `review_required`, `review_reasons` `wht_not_withheld`, `not_withheld_payments` and `review_en` / `review_ar` for the tax adviser; a reversed payment gives the negative row in the reversal month, flagged as in B5.
25. **Lock order (item 3).** `approve_deferred_sale_rebate` locks the entity, the contract, then the rebate; `_refresh_rebates` locked the rebates first and then the contract (PostgreSQL deadlock with an approval of the same rebate). It now reads the candidates without a lock, locks each contract before its rebate (ordered by contract) and reads the rebate again under the lock (skipped when no longer approved with a non-final note). PostgreSQL test with two threads (the reviewer's D1).
26. **What the rebating token protects (item 4; invoicing R128 note).** The token, grant and matrix checks of `invoicing_port.rebate_problems` protect against mistakes (wrong caller, rebate, amount or person, a replay after the approval), not against code in the same process that can write the draft rebate row and obtain the approver's grant (the reviewer's p2). The guard is that only `payroll.services.approve_deferred_sale_rebate` calls `invoicing_port.rebating` and the port's `create_bai_ajel_rebate`, and only `invoicing.payroll_port` calls `invoicing.services.create_deferred_sale_adjustment`: a test parses every non-test module (names, attributes and `getattr` strings) and fails on any other caller.
27. **A bai' ajel balance left after exit, written off or recovered (item 5; OQ-33 / OQ-34 team defaults, applied by the owner's instruction "proceed on the recommendations").** `DeferredSaleClosure` (kinds `write_off`, `recovery`): `prepare_deferred_sale_closure(contract, *, user, kind, reason_ar, reason_en="", amount=None, expense_account=None, bank_account=None, received_on=None, bank_reference="")` by the payroll officer (`payroll.prepare_loan_write_off`, as loans) → `submit_deferred_sale_closure` → `approve_deferred_sale_closure` or `reject_deferred_sale_closure` (reason in Arabic) by the holder of the existing write-off right `payroll.approve_loan_write_off` under the matrix row `payroll.loan_write_off` (the managing partner in Almiras, the CEO elsewhere, as loan write-offs; never the preparer, submitter, last modifier or the employee). Only after the employee's final settlement is approved, with nothing planned left, no rebate open (`rebate_open`: discard it) and no rebate credit note pending (`rebate_pending`); one open closure per contract (`closure_exists`, constraint `payroll_one_open_sale_closure`); the balance is checked again at approval under the entity and contract locks (`closure_amount`). Posting only through the ledger registry (`payroll.deferredsaleclosure`, `ledger.restricted` allowances for 1240 and, write-off only, 1241):
    - **write-off** of the whole balance, dated the approval day: Dr 1241 the profit not yet recognised (`profit_share`, read from invoicing through the port's `unearned_bai_ajel_profit` at preparation and again at approval; refused when it does not fit the balance, `closure_profit`), Dr the write-off account (the settings' loan write-off account, default 5100, as loans; a postable expense account of the entity, not Sharia-prohibited) the rest, Cr 1240; the installments become `written_off`.
    - **recovery** (the former employee paid; invoicing's customer receipts never settle a bai' ajel invoice, so payroll records it), up to the balance, 2 decimals, into ~~a postable asset account of the entity~~ *(amended 2026-10-21, review 5 item 1)* a **bank or cash account** of the entity (1100 or a sub-account of 1100, the banking R5 bank GL rule; 1110 petty cash or a sub-account of it; postable and active; never a `ledger.restricted` account such as 1140, and never 1240, 1241 or 1250: `bank_account_invalid`, checked at preparation, at approval and in `_closure_lines`, which never debits the receivable), received on a date not in the future *and not before the sale date or the approval date of the employee's final settlement (review 5 item 4, `recovery_date`; checked again at approval)*, dated that day: Dr bank / Cr 1240; the installments it covers, oldest first, become `recovered_after_exit` (a part-covered one is reduced to what was recovered and its remainder stays `outstanding_after_exit`, `carried_from` it), and invoicing recognises the profit share of each through the port inside `invoicing_port.collecting` of the approved recovery (E13; the collection completing the receivable releases the rest).
    *Amended 2026-10-21 (review 5):* a write-off stores `vat_share`, the VAT included in the balance written off (balance x the sale's VAT / receivable, less rebates whose note is final), which stays on 2200; the approver sees an en/ar warning (`warnings`) and `bai_ajel_write_off_vat_list(entity, *, user, start=None, end=None)` (`payroll.view`) lists the approved write-offs with VAT for the tax adviser (bad-debt relief, invoicing OQ-78). The approval first reads the contract's pending rebate notes in its own committed step and is refused with `rebate_rejected_retry` (en/ar) when one was rejected meanwhile (item 35). PostgreSQL (`payroll.0024`): the installments move to `written_off` / `recovered_after_exit` only when an approved closure lists them; rejected closures are locked (item 37).
    The contract becomes `settled` when nothing planned or outstanding is left; each approval is logged (AccessLog `bai_ajel_write_off` / `bai_ajel_recovery`, en/ar). PostgreSQL (`payroll.0020`): an installment outstanding after exit moves only to `written_off` (status only) or `recovered_after_exit` (status, the amount reduced to what was recovered, `deducted_amount` = amount); both are final and never deleted; an approved closure only receives its journal entry once and is never deleted.
28. **Alerts job (item 2).** `send_alerts` runs each Saudi entity in two transactions, the bai' ajel refresh (its rebate restores, statuses and alerts committing together, item 18) apart from the Mudad, GOSI, leave and settlement alerts (`_entity_alerts`); a failing step is logged and the job goes on with the other steps and entities, then raises `AlertsFailed` (en/ar, `alerts_failed`, the entities and steps, what was sent); the `payroll_alerts` command prints them and exits non-zero (`CommandError`, return code 1).
29. **Tests (item 7).** `payroll.0019` and `0020` reverse to `0018` and re-apply on PostgreSQL (columns, trigger functions and triggers checked both ways); the reviewer's p3 (a new rebate after exit is refused at approval) and p4 (a rejection read twice after exit restores once) are kept as tests.

Fifth review: verification of the bai' ajel closures and the payroll WHT cap (2026-10-21, as built; owner: "adopt the recommendations"; tests `payroll/test_review_fixes_2026_10_21b.py` (fake port, ported from the reviewer's rv5 probes), `invoicing/test_deferred_sale_review5.py` (real port) and `payroll/test_pg_review_2026_10_21b.py` (PostgreSQL); migrations `payroll.0024`, `invoicing.0033`):
30. **Recovery into a bank or cash account only (item 1, MAJOR).** A recovery could debit 1240 itself (recognising profit with no cash) or 1250. `_bank_account` (payments and recoveries) now also requires a bank or cash account: 1100 or a sub-account of 1100 (banking R5; the chart has no bank/cash flag), 1110 or a sub-account of it, active, never a `ledger.restricted` account and never 1240, 1241 or 1250 (`bank_account_invalid`, en/ar); `_closure_lines` refuses a recovery debiting the receivable whoever calls it. A payroll payment is held to the same rule.
31. **Fully withheld payment (item 2, MAJOR).** When the cap of item 23 makes the WHT equal to the whole payment (a payee with a 0.01 net paid alone; a settlement of gross 100,000.00 and net 100.00 paid 40.00 then 60.00), the bank line was 0.00 and the ledger refused the entry. The bank line is now omitted: Dr 2330 / Cr 2220 (and any realised FX difference), and the payment carries an en/ar note that it is recorded as fully withheld. The settlement becomes `paid` when the approved payments reach its due amount, as before; the WHT return ties out.
32. **Held-back profit caught up on a rejected rebate note (item 3; OQ-33; invoicing R128(b)).** A write-off's `profit_share` depended on whether a recovery came first, because the profit held back while a rebate note was pending was released only by the next collection. On the rejection, `_refresh_rebates` now asks invoicing (port `catch_up_bai_ajel_profit`, inside `invoicing_port.catching_up` for that rejected rebate, approver named) to recognise the held-back profit on what is already collected, posting only through the ledger. Then the profit not yet recognised is the profit on what is still outstanding: write-off straight and recover-then-write-off end with 1241 at zero and differ only by the recovery itself (its profit share on 4610, its cash on the bank, the cash less that share on 5100).
33. **Recovery date (item 4).** `received_on` is on or after the contract's sale date and the approval date of the employee's final settlement (`recovery_date`, en/ar), at preparation and at approval.
34. **VAT share of a write-off (item 5; invoicing OQ-78).** `DeferredSaleClosure.vat_share` (constraint `payroll_sale_closure_vat_share`: 0 to the amount) and `warnings`; the en/ar warning to the approver; `bai_ajel_write_off_vat_list` for the tax adviser; logged with the approval (`vat_share`). Nothing posts to 2200.
35. **A rebate note rejected at approval (item 6).** The approval read the pending notes inside its own transaction: a rejection found there was rolled back with the refused approval, so the user was stuck. `approve_deferred_sale_closure` now checks the right, reads the contract's pending notes in its own committed step (`_refresh_rebates`), and raises `rebate_rejected_retry` (en/ar: discard the rebate, reject this closure, prepare a new one for the new balance) when one was rejected; otherwise the approval transaction runs as before (`_approve_deferred_sale_closure`).
36. **Labels (item 7).** `payroll.approve_loan_write_off` reads "Approve and post loan and bai' ajel write-offs and recoveries" / "اعتماد شطب القروض والبيع الآجل واستردادها وترحيلها"; the matrix row `payroll.loan_write_off` is "loan and bai' ajel write-offs and recoveries" / "شطب القروض والبيع الآجل واستردادها"; access.md's activity row names `approve_deferred_sale_closure`. A separate activity comes with the authority-matrix branch (no core seed migration now).
37. **Database guards (item 8, `payroll.0024`).** `payroll_installment_lock`: a bai' ajel installment outstanding after exit becomes `written_off` only when an approved write-off of its contract and entity lists it in `installment_parts`, and `recovered_after_exit` only when an approved recovery lists it at that amount (the approval stores the closure approved with its parts before it moves the installments); loan installments unchanged. `payroll_sale_closure_lock` (its own function now): never deleted; approved, only its journal entry once; rejected, never changed. Reverses to `0023` (0020's function and trigger).
38. **Tests (item 9).** The cap in a 3-decimal currency (KWD: a 0.005 net, fully withheld at 0.005, SAR 0.06); partial recovery then write-off on the real port; two concurrent approvals of the same closure on PostgreSQL post once (the other `closure_status`); the reviewer's p3 (a closure entry is never reversed through the ledger directly), p5 and p6 kept as tests.

Not covered (open): an ibra' after the employee's final settlement (the balance outstanding after exit is not reduced by a rebate; it is written off or recovered, item 27); the written-off VAT part stays on 2200 (VAT bad-debt relief is an adviser question with invoicing OQ-78; *2026-10-21:* stored as `vat_share` and listed for the adviser, item 34); a recovery in a currency other than SAR, and reversing an approved closure, are not built; who bears the WHT not withheld (items 23, 24) is for the tax adviser (OQ-29); the `draft` → `approved` transition of a rebate is not checked by the trigger (the service checks it); the rebate figures in `sale_state.receivable` stay the sale's own receivable (payroll does not read them).

Open (left for the advisers, defaults stand): Sharia: OQ-33 (ibra' allowed, possession, agency), OQ-75 (on-collection vs periodic recognition); legal: OQ-34 (EOSB as security), OQ-38, OQ-48; tax: OQ-29 (WHT on non-resident remuneration, category, rate), invoicing OQ-77 (RETT), OQ-78 (VAT on the profit); Egypt adviser: the research rows (OQ-26, OQ-28) including the minimum wage, the debt caps and the "fewer than 10 workers" exemption; DPO: invoicing OQ-74 (names).

## 11. Slice 3 (to build) (2026-10-20)

Written 2026-10-20 by the spec-writer agent, on the owner's instruction of 2026-10-20: "start payroll slice 3", proceed on the team's recommendations, no questions. Where the team had to choose, the choice is a **default** and is listed in 11.9 with its recommendation. Nothing here is a new owner decision.

### 11.1 Scope

| # | In scope | Section |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Off-cycle runs for the `out_of_country` group (remuneration staff) and for Egypt (`local_entity_employee` in an EG entity) | R69 to R74, E16, E17 |
| 2 | Leave liability report (accrued, untaken annual leave per employee at the Labour Law basis) and EOSB provision report (Art. 84) per entity as at a date. Reports only, nothing posted (hr OQ-6 still open) | R75 to R78 |
| 3 | Mudad justifications as data on run lines (payslips), exported with the WPS file where the format allows | R79 to R81 |
| 4 | Payroll approvals routed through the shared approval engine / authority matrix (access §3.8 to §3.10, R57 to R62, and access §10 being written): what payroll calls, and the fallback when no matrix row exists | R82 to R84 |

**Out of scope:** per-bank WPS byte layouts (P18, formats awaited, OQ-10); Egypt NOSI / ETA filings and payments (no payment kind; the payables stay on their accounts); leave accrual postings (2350 / 5130 stay unused, hr OQ-6); bai' ajel asset returns (§10.9 A8: refused).

**Applies to every entity** as data: an entity runs slice 3 features through its `PayrollSettings`, its jurisdiction's activated and verified parameters (R4, hr R68) and its matrix rows. No code path names Almiras; "Almiras" below means the entity whose data says so (group parent, overtime route `managing_partner`).

### 11.2 Decisions used

| Source | What it settles here | Date |
|---|---|---|
| Owner, "start payroll slice 3 … proceed on the team's recommendations, no questions" | Build items 1 to 4; defaults as in 11.9 | 2026-10-20 |
| P8, P9, P16 | Remuneration staff: foreign currency, WHT, outside the Labour Law and WPS | 2026-10-17 |
| P10 | Groups `in_country` / `out_of_country`, cut-offs 27th / 6th | 2026-10-17 |
| P19(b) | GOSI for subordinate Saudi remuneration staff: annuities only | 2026-10-17 |
| P19(f) | Only Egypt activated besides KSA | 2026-10-17 |
| P20(a), (b), (c) = owner answers A2, A3, A4 | Per-entity authority matrix; contracted payroll without the MP; Almiras overtime by the MP; self-approval only by the top authority; no cascade | 2026-10-18 |
| Owner answer A10 | All approvable documents go through one approval engine | 2026-10-18 |
| Owner answer E4 | Egypt rules: the team researches them (`docs/compliance/egypt-payroll-2026-10-18.md`, all rows unverified) | 2026-10-18 |
| hr OQ-6 (open) | Leave is not accrued in the books; "liability report only" (hr default) | 2026-10-17 |

### 11.3 Data model (additions)

Conventions as §3 (entity FK `PROTECT`, `created_by`, `source`, Bi = `_en` / `_ar`, Money in `entity.base_currency` at 2 dp, transaction amounts at the currency's own decimals).

**11.3.1 `PayrollRun` / `Payslip` (changed, no new model).** `kind=off_cycle` is now allowed for `payroll_group=out_of_country` and for in-country runs of an entity whose `HrSettings.jurisdiction` is `EG`. The off-cycle purposes built for KSA (`leave_advance`, maternity settlement) stay KSA-only; the new runs use the general off-cycle purpose already built (pay what is still due). New field:

| Model | Field | Type | Req | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `PayrollRun` | `entry_date_reason` | Char(30), null | N | `period_closed` when the entry moves to the first day of the next open period (R70) |

**11.3.2 `MudadJustification` (new; one per payslip and reason; not registered in the ledger)**

| Field | Type | Req | Bi | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| entity | FK Entity | Y | | Same as the payslip |
| payslip | FK Payslip, PROTECT | Y | | The "run line" it explains; `in_country` KSA payslips only (R79) |
| run / wps_file | FK PayrollRun / FK WpsFile, null | Y (N for a proactive row) / N | | ~~`wps_file` set when exported~~ *(2026-10-21, C3: justifications never travel in a salary file; the field stays empty, kept for the history of 0021)* |
| employee_code | Char | Y | | Copied; no name |
| kind | Choice `non_payment`, `partial_payment`, `amount_difference`, `late_payment` | Y | | |
| reason | Choice (internal list, 11.6) | Y | | |
| ~~mudad_code~~ mudad_option_ar / mudad_option_en | Char(200) | N | Y | *Amended 2026-10-21 (slice 3 review and compliance check, owner: adopt the recommendations):* the Mudad portal drop-down option (compliance C3), from `wps.mudad.justification_options` **[verify: Mudad option list]**; empty while the parameter is empty. Proactive rows (C11) have `employee`, `from_period`, `to_period` and no payslip |
| expected_amount / paid_amount | Money / Money | Y | | Expected = contract monthly wage of the period (R79); paid = payslip ~~net~~ **gross** (MOL-ALL, R79(d), AT-83; amended 2026-10-21) (0 for non-payment) |
| description_ar / description_en | Text(500) | Y / N | Y | Arabic required. No diagnosis or health detail (R80) |
| evidence_ref | Char(200), null | N | | Leave request, court order, bank return code, etc. |
| mudad_notice_date | Date, null | N | | Date of Mudad's notice for this employee and month |
| due_date / worker_response_due | Date / Date | computed | | notice + 10 days / submission + 3 days (R53) |
| worker_response | Choice `none`, `agreed`, `disputed` | Y | | Default `none` |
| status | Choice `suggested`, `draft`, `approved`, `submitted`, `accepted`, `rejected`, `withdrawn` | Y | | R80 |
| created_by / approved_by / approved_at / submitted_on / mudad_reference | | | | Recording as `MudadSubmission` (manual) |

`MudadSubmission.justifications` (JSON, 3.9) is read-only from slice 3; a data migration copies its entries into `MudadJustification` rows (`source=import`, status `submitted`).

**11.3.3 `PayrollLiabilityReport` and `PayrollLiabilityReportLine` (new; snapshots, append-only)**

| Field | Type | Req | Bi | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| entity | FK Entity | Y | | One entity per report |
| kind | Choice `leave`, `eosb` | Y | | |
| as_of | Date | Y | | ≤ today (Riyadh) |
| basis_en / basis_ar | Text | Y | Y | Frozen copy of the basis and assumptions text (R76, R77) |
| parameter_ids / contract_version_ids | JSON | Y | | Everything read |
| total_days / total_amount | Decimal(9,2) / Money | Y | | |
| generated_by / generated_at / sha256 | | Y | | Hash of header and lines |
| status | Choice `generated`, `superseded` | Y | | A later report of the same entity, kind and `as_of` supersedes |

Line: entity, report, employee (FK), employee_code, employment_basis, jurisdiction, `valuation` (`valued`, `not_valued`, `excluded`), reason (Bi), leave: `balance_days`, `pro_rata_days`, `future_leave_days`, `untaken_days`, `daily_wage`; EOSB: `service_start`, `service_days`, `excluded_days`, `wage_basis`, `art84_full_award`, `art85_resignation_memo`; both: `amount`, `subledger_balance_memo` (EOSB only).

**11.3.4 Parameters (new, all seeded `unverified`)**

| Code | Jurisdiction | Seed | Note |
|---|---|---|---|
| ~~`wps.mudad.justification_codes`~~ `wps.mudad.justification_options` | SA | empty | Internal reason → Mudad portal option (ar / en) **[verify: Mudad option list, not in HRSD sources]** (amended 2026-10-21, C3) |
| `mudad.health_words` | SA | team list | PDPL screen of justification texts and evidence **[verify: DPO]** (2026-10-21) |
| `eosb.in_kind_housing` | SA | 3 months of BASIC a year | Art. 2 in-kind housing in the EOSB wage **[verify: legal]** (2026-10-21, C4) |
| `si.wage_reference` | EG | `monthly` (option `january`) | Egypt insurable wage reference **[verify: Egypt adviser]** (2026-10-21, C10) |
| ~~`wps.format.<bank>.justification_fields`~~ | | | *Removed 2026-10-21 (C3): the HRSD WPS guide has justifications entered on the Mudad portal through a drop-down, never in the salary file* |

### 11.4 Rules

Enforcement as §4 (**svc**, **M**, **DB**, **Q**, **L**, **T**, **job**); **eng** = approval engine adapter.

**Off-cycle runs: common**
69. **Off-cycle `out_of_country` and Egypt runs.** `prepare_run(kind="off_cycle")` is accepted for the `out_of_country` group and for an EG entity's in-country group, with `reason_ar` and named employees of that group. It is refused (en/ar `regular_run_open`) until the period's regular run of the same entity and group is approved. It pays only what is still due for the named employees: a payslip refused or excluded in the regular run (`exclude_refused`, R56), and approved inputs of the period or earlier not yet taken (§10.5 inputs rule; not bound by the cut-off day). Like the KSA off-cycle: no loan or bai' ajel instalments, no EOSB accrual, no re-examination of earlier months; an employee with nothing due is left out with a count warning. Runs are approved through the matrix activity `payroll.run` (R82). Agents and system users never prepare or approve them (R38). **[svc; AT-73, AT-75, AT-78]**
70. **Entry date, rate and netting.** The entry is dated the last day of the period when that fiscal period is open, else the first day of the next open period (`entry_date_reason=period_closed`, en/ar warning), as the final settlement (§10.6 item 11). Remuneration lines use the ledger rate of the entry date (R42). Every amount is netted against the period's approved lines of the same entity, group and employee (§10.5 netting): only the difference is paid. A payslip whose contract currency differs from a payslip already approved for that period is refused (en/ar, naming both currencies; §10.9 item 8). Each payslip has its own fingerprint and stored hash (R8, §10.5). **[svc + L; AT-73, AT-75]**

**Off-cycle runs: `out_of_country`**
71. **GOSI.** Non-Saudi and `non_subordinate` Saudi: no GOSI lines. `subordinate` Saudi: annuities only (no OH, no SANED, R41), on the month's `REMUNERATION` amount in SAR (R62) **not yet covered** by an approved payslip of the period; floor and cap (R20, R68) apply to the period's GOSI wage across all its payslips. One-off earnings and bonuses of remuneration staff carry no GOSI (R62 base = `REMUNERATION` only; default, OQ-50). `legacy_m33` keeps the R41 warning. **[svc; AT-73, AT-74]**
72. **Exclusions and WHT.** No WPS file and no Mudad record or alert for an `out_of_country` run (R51; `generate_wps_file` refused, en/ar `wps_not_applicable`); no Labour Law caps, overtime, leave or EOSB (R41); net ≥ 0. WHT is taken at payment (R43): the `net_salaries` payment names the off-cycle payslips, needs a WHT category (OQ-29), and its WHT base is each payee's gross in the share of their net paid. **[svc; AT-74]**

**Off-cycle runs: Egypt**
73. **Egypt calculation.** EG rows only (never an SA row, hr R51), in EGP, as §10.8 item 7, on the **month combined** (the period's approved EG payslips plus this run): (a) social insurance 11% / 18.75% on the insurable components (`si.insurable_components`) of the month, clamped to the year's `si.wage_limits`, minus what the period already booked; a month already at the maximum gets none; (b) UHI netted the same way where `work_governorate` is live; the employer minimum (EGP 50) applies once per month, never again in an off-cycle run; (c) Martyrs' Fund 0.05% of this run's gross (default employee-borne, research C8); (d) salary tax by the cumulative method (`tax.salary.method`): the year-to-date tax on year-to-date taxable pay including this run, minus tax already withheld this year; a negative result is not refunded in the off-cycle run (en/ar warning; the next regular run's cumulative true-up handles it, default OQ-51); (e) Egypt deduction caps on the combined month (nafaqa ≤ 50% first, other debts ≤ 25%, loans ≤ 10%; a fixed deduction above them refuses the payslip). Activation: calculated with a warning but refused at submission and approval while any EG row it read is unverified (as the regular EG run). Accounts as §10.8 item 7 (CFO to approve dedicated Egypt accounts). **[svc + parameter; AT-76, AT-77]**
74. **Egypt exclusions.** No EOSB accrual (`eosb.monthly_accrual` false), no GOSI, no WPS file, no Mudad; overtime inputs stay refused. An employee whose contract ends in or before the period, or who has an approved termination, is refused (en/ar `eg_final_settlement_not_built`): an off-cycle run is never used as a final settlement. NOSI, UHI and ETA payables stay on 2340 / 2300 / 2220 until a payment kind exists (out of scope); only `net_salaries` payments are recorded. **[svc; AT-78]**

**Liability reports (no posting)**
75. **Reports, not postings.** `leave_liability_report(entity, as_of, *, user)` and `eosb_provision_report(entity, as_of, *, user)` (`payroll.prepare_liability_report`) build a `PayrollLiabilityReport` snapshot and post nothing: no journal entry, no `EosbProvisionEntry`, no `LeaveLedger` row; 2350 and 5130 are never used (hr OQ-6). Totals need `payroll.view_cost_reports`; per-employee lines need `payroll.view_payslip` **as well** (*Amended 2026-10-21 (slice 3 review and compliance check, owner: adopt the recommendations):* `payroll.view_payslip` alone gives nothing) in that entity (each view logged, PDPL; §10.5 reports rule). One entity per report, through `scope`; another entity's report → 404; no cross-entity total without a membership in each entity (no cascade, access R64). `as_of` after today is refused (en/ar). Snapshots are never edited; a new one supersedes. Output HTML and CSV, Arabic first, "the Arabic text prevails" (R40); codes only, no names or ID numbers in the CSV. **[svc + Q + DB guard; AT-79, AT-80, AT-81]**
76. **Leave liability basis (KSA `ksa_employee`).** Per employee and `ANNUAL` leave type: `untaken_days` = `LeaveLedger` balance from rows dated on or before `as_of` + `pro_rata_days` (entitlement / 12 × days of the open month to `as_of` / days in that month, computed in memory, not written; hr R30) + `future_leave_days` (approved annual leave days falling after `as_of` whose `taken` row is dated on or before it, hr R31). Days whose pay was already advanced by a leave advance run (§10.5) stay in the liability: the advance is a salary prepayment, not the leave itself (default, OQ-52). Value = `untaken_days` × daily wage, daily wage = Σ components with `counts_for_eosb` in the contract version in force on `as_of` / 30 (Labour Law Art. 2, 111; same basis as encashment, R34). No GOSI or other employer cost on top (OQ-24 default). Undiscounted, no interest element (Sharia neutral). Excluded with a reason: `remuneration` (no Saudi leave, P16); terminated employees whose final settlement is approved (their leave was encashed). `local_entity_employee` of EG: days shown, `valuation=not_valued` until `leave.encashment_basis` (EG) is set and verified (OQ-55); other jurisdictions: excluded (not activated). The basis text (en/ar) above is printed on the report. **[svc; AT-79, AT-80]**
77. **EOSB provision report (Art. 84).** Per `ksa_employee` employed on `as_of`: `art84_full_award` = `hr.calculate_eosb(employee, as_of=as_of, kind=full)` (Labour Law Art. 84: half a month's wage per year for the first five years, one month per later year, part years pro rata by days, P19(h); fraction 1, the provision basis of R24 and OQ-11), on the actual wage of the version in force on `as_of`, service excluding suspended unpaid days (hr R62). Memo columns, never a tie-out and never posted: `art85_resignation_memo` (Art. 85 fraction for a resignation at `as_of`: 0 under 2 years, 1/3 for 2 to 5 years inclusive, 2/3 for more than 5 and under 10, 1 from 10 (wording corrected 2026-10-21, C8); default, OQ-53) and `subledger_balance_memo` (the employee's `EosbProvisionEntry` balance on `as_of`), with the entity total of 2500 on `as_of` shown once for information. Undiscounted vested amounts; no actuarial or discount factor (OQ-11 default; Sharia neutral). Excluded with a reason: `remuneration` (no EOSB, P16), Egypt and other non-SA staff (no statutory gratuity configured, `eosb.monthly_accrual` false; the report states it in en/ar), employees with an approved final settlement (*Amended 2026-10-21 (slice 3 review and compliance check, owner: adopt the recommendations):* one whose last working day is on or before `as_of`). *Amended 2026-10-21 (slice 3 review and compliance check, owner: adopt the recommendations):* a leaver without an approved settlement is valued at the last working day with the fraction of the actual termination kind, marked "due, settlement pending" (en/ar); the wage includes the Art. 2 regular additions (C4: the last 12 months' average of regular commissions and bonuses, in-kind housing), also in the monthly provision and the leave report. An hr refusal for one employee (e.g. `no_terms`) gives `not_valued` with the reason; other refusals stop the report (as `_eosb`, §10.5). **[svc + hr; AT-81, AT-82]**
78. **Basis statement.** Each report header carries, in English and Arabic: the as-at date, the law basis (Art. 2, 84, 111; P19(h)), "report only: nothing is posted; leave accrual is not decided (hr OQ-6)", the excluded groups and their count, and the parameter ids read. **[svc + T; AT-79, AT-81]**

**Mudad justifications**
79. **What needs a justification.** For each approved `in_country` run of an SA entity with WPS (R53), the service `suggest_mudad_justifications(run)` (called at WPS generation and when the bank-processed file is attached) writes `suggested` rows (`source=system`, never approved by a system user) for each KSA employee in the run where: (a) the employee is in the WPS exception list (no valid IBAN, IBAN changed, termination approved; §10.5) → `non_payment`; (b) the bank-processed file gives a rejection `RET-CDE` → `non_payment`, reason `bank_rejected`, `evidence_ref` = the return code; (c) the payslip was refused or excluded (R56) → `non_payment`, reason `paid_off_cycle` or `payroll_correction`; (d) the payslip's gross (MOL-ALL) differs from the contract monthly wage of the period (Σ fixed components of the version in force at the period start) → `amount_difference` or `partial_payment`, reason from the payslip lines (unpaid leave, sick tier, maternity `pending_gosi` / top-up, joiner / leaver proration, contract suspended); (e) the pay date is more than a month after the previous run's (Art. 90 warning, §10.5) → `late_payment`. `remuneration` and EG payslips never get a justification (refused, en/ar `mudad_not_applicable`). The payroll officer may also create rows by hand (`payroll.record_mudad_justification`). **[svc + job; AT-83, AT-84]**
80. **Approval and deadlines.** A `suggested` row becomes `draft` when the officer confirms or edits it; `approve_mudad_justification` (`payroll.approve_mudad_justification`, **A**; matrix activity `payroll.mudad_justification`, code default `hr_officer`) never by its creator or the employee. Only approved rows are exported or recorded as `submitted` (with `submitted_on`, `mudad_reference`), then `accepted` / `rejected` from Mudad (manual, R32). `due_date` = `mudad_notice_date` + 10 days; `worker_response_due` = `submitted_on` + 3 days (R53, WPS-G). Alerts (en/ar) to the payroll officers and hr_officers 3 days before `due_date`, on it, and the day after while not `submitted`. `description_ar` must not hold a diagnosis or health detail: the reason code carries "sick leave" or "maternity leave" only; the form shows that instruction en/ar and the export contains only the code and the approved text (PDPL; hr R11). Rows are never deleted; a wrong one is `withdrawn` with a reason. **[svc + job + M; AT-83, AT-84]**
81. **Export.** `export_mudad_justifications(run)` (`payroll.generate_wps_file`, not segregated, see below) gives a bilingual list (CSV and HTML) per run of approved rows: employee code, MOL-ID (as in the WPS file, encrypted at rest, logged), period, kind, internal reason, the Mudad portal option (`mudad_option_ar` / `_en`), expected, paid, Arabic text, evidence reference, due date. ~~When the bank's format parameter declares `justification_fields`, the same code and text are also written into those columns of the WPS file~~ *Amended 2026-10-21 (slice 3 review and compliance check, owner: adopt the recommendations):* the HRSD WPS guide has justifications chosen from a drop-down on the Mudad portal within 10 days of the upload (the worker then has 3 days), never in the salary file: the export is the officer's **worksheet** for portal entry (with the portal option, `mudad_option_ar` / `_en`, and the due date), not segregated from the preparer (the S! question is moot: decided non-segregated); the WPS file never carries justifications (C3). A row without a portal option is exported with an en/ar warning ("Mudad portal option not configured"). Exports are kept like WPS files (R31, kept indefinitely, burn only with the committee minute). **[svc + parameter; AT-83, AT-85]**

**Approvals through the shared engine (dependency on access)**
82. **What payroll calls.** Every payroll approval is reached only through the engine (access R61): payroll registers one adapter per approvable type and calls `approvals.submit(document, user)` from its submit services; the engine's final eligible decision calls payroll's own approve service with the single-use, document-bound grant (access R30, R31); return and reject call payroll's return / discard services. Each adapter supplies: `activity` (table below), `amount_basis_value(document)` in the functional currency (SAR; EGP for EG entities), `document_version(document)` = payroll's stored hash (§10.5, so an engine decision on an older version is refused, `recalculate`), and a bilingual label with the document number and period only (no names, no individual pay; R36). Payroll's own checks stay in payroll and run on every decision: the fingerprint and stored-hash checks (R8), the own-inputs rule (R39, R50), the employee ≠ approver rule for settlements, corrections and write-offs, and the 3 × BASIC loan cap (R46). **[eng + svc; T: every payroll **A** permission is reached only from an engine adapter or the fallback seam; AT-86, AT-87]**

| Activity | Permission | Code default when the entity has no row (R83) | Amount basis (default, OQ-54) |
|---|---|---|---|
| `payroll.run` (regular and off-cycle) | `payroll.approve_run` | `hr_officer`; never `managing_partner` ("contracted payroll needs no MP approval", P20(a)) | `none` |
| `payroll.run_reversal` | `payroll.reverse_run` | `approver` | `none` |
| `payroll.input` | `payroll.approve_input` | `payroll_officer` (not own entry, not own pay) | `none` |
| `payroll.overtime` | `payroll.approve_overtime` | `HrSettings.overtime_approval_route`: `managing_partner` (Almiras, decided P20(a)) or `department_head`; a head's own overtime or no head → the entity's top authority | `none` |
| `payroll.loan` | `payroll.approve_loan` | The entity's top authority (`EntityGovernance.top_authority_role`: MP in Almiras, `ceo` elsewhere) | principal |
| `payroll.deferred_sale_terms` / `payroll.deferred_sale` (incl. the ibra' rebate, §10.9 A8) | `payroll.approve_deferred_sale_terms` / `payroll.approve_deferred_sale` | Top authority | sale price / rebate |
| `payroll.final_settlement` / `payroll.settlement_correction` | `payroll.approve_final_settlement` / `payroll.approve_settlement_correction` | `approver` | net / absolute change of net |
| `payroll.loan_write_off` | `payroll.approve_loan_write_off` | Top authority | amount |
| `payroll.payment` / `payroll.payment_reversal` | `payroll.approve_payment` / `payroll.reverse_payment` | `approver` | amount |
| `payroll.gosi_fine` | `payroll.approve_gosi_fine` | `approver` | amount |
| `payroll.cost_item` / `payroll.secondment_cost_report` | `payroll.approve_cost_item` / `payroll.approve_secondment_cost_report` | `approver` | amount / `none` |
| `payroll.maternity_clearance` | `payroll.approve_maternity_clearance` | `approver` | `none` |
| `payroll.mudad_justification` (new) | `payroll.approve_mudad_justification` (new) | `hr_officer` | `none` |

Activities not yet in the access catalogue (3.8) are added by the access build: `payroll.run_reversal`, `payroll.input`, `payroll.final_settlement`, `payroll.settlement_correction`, `payroll.loan_write_off`, `payroll.payment`, `payroll.payment_reversal`, `payroll.gosi_fine`, `payroll.secondment_cost_report`, `payroll.mudad_justification`. The exact permission codenames are payroll's as built (§10); a mismatch with the access catalogue is fixed in the catalogue, not by renaming payroll permissions.

83. **Fallback when no matrix row exists.** As access R57 as amended 2026-10-20 (access §10, R70, R71, being written; **access governs where it differs**): (a) the entity has **no row at all** for the activity → the code default of the table above (today's `payroll/approvals.py` behaviour), checked with `require` and logged as `code_default`; (b) the entity has rows but the amount falls in a **gap** inside its bands → submission refused (en/ar `no_authority_rule`, naming entity, activity and amount), the document stays draft; (c) an eligible role has no active holder → the document waits (access R67) and the daily alerts job tells the payroll officers (en/ar) after 2 days (default). Never: a fallback to a group role, to the MP outside an entity where the MP has a membership (no cascade, P20(c)), or to the MP for `payroll.run` unless that entity's own rows name `managing_partner`. **Until the engine exists** (access build), payroll keeps its seam `payroll/approvals.py`: the same code defaults, the same refusals, no self-approval; switching to the engine needs no payroll change beyond registering the adapters. **[eng + svc; AT-86, AT-88]**
84. **Self-approval and the governance report.** Payroll never passes `allow_self_approval` itself. Only the engine's top-authority path (access R58) may let the entity's top authority approve a payroll document they created, submitted, modified or entered inputs in as maker (R39, R50 amended); never a document of their own benefit, such as a run in which their own payslip has inputs or retro lines, a loan, settlement or payment to themselves (access R79(e), R82; clarified 2026-10-21 after the review of the access §10 build, item 5); the `hr_officer`, `approver`, `payroll_officer` and a delegate never self-approve (access R58, R62). Not for the S! export (R81). Each such decision appears in the monthly governance report (access R59) with payroll's label (R82). **[eng + svc; AT-87]**
